(`runs/highmem/`). The 24 h extension (`runs/xb/`, user-requested, Amendment 5) completed all six systems in
44–54 min each, peak RSS 16.4–17.9 GiB: m = 3 degree-2 S = 32 refuted at 5.10·10¹² F4 mults (×320 per doubling
from S = 16); A3 at S = 27 r = 0.45 (gain); part B algebraic/enumeration 5.0·10⁷ at S = 32, 36.
`analyze_extension.py` → `results_extension.json`; REPORT and tutorial updated. Pod deleted (~6.9 h, ~$7).

## Why m = 3 S = 32 and S = 36 cost the same

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Added `--trace 1` to `examples/pkm_odd_tower_f4.rs` (F4 per-step log; totals unchanged). Two RunPod pods
(128 GiB, deleted after use): traced reruns of both systems (`runs/trace/`, totals reproduce exactly) and a
coincidence test (`runs/coinc/`: seed 77 at P1, and P2). Counts are invariant across curve, coset, target and
prime (to ~1e-9, schedule noise); the ratio stays 0.999768. Traces differ in every step (24 vs 34 steps, top
degree 7 vs 8). Report section added.

### Decisions made (and why)

Called the match a coincidence between two shape constants: reproducible, but no common mechanism found.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

A closed form for F4's count as a function of the degree sequence would settle it.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

None. Pods: ~1 h + ~1.4 h at $1.04/h (study total ~$10 of the $85 ceiling).

## 2026-11-15T00:10:00Z — Q&A: why DLP solvers are faster on genus ≥ 2 Jacobians

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Answered a conceptual question in chat; no code, data or experiment touched.
- Summarised: Mumford decomposition gives a natural smoothness notion with
  constant probability 1/g! and a q-sized factor base against a q^g group;
  Gaudry / Thériault / GTTD Õ(q^{2-2/g}) beats rho q^{g/2} for g ≥ 3 [pcc:unverified] and ties
  at g = 2; Diem's line-based relations give Õ(q) on non-hyperelliptic genus 3
  (plane quartics), Diem–Kochinke Õ(q^{2-2/(g-1)}) for g ≥ 4; Smith's trigonal
  isogeny transfers ~18.6% of hyperelliptic genus-3 instances to that case;
  large genus goes L(1/2) then L(1/3) (Enge–Gaudry, EGT, Diem), which is what
  makes Weil descent (GHS) dangerous for E(F_{q^n}).
- Sources checked: answered from standard literature; KB not queried
  (conceptual question, no lead or novelty claim made).

### Decisions made (and why)

- Flagged for the program: a prime-field ECDLP transferred into genus 2 gains
  no exponent (Õ(q) vs rho Õ(q)); only genus ≥ 3, ideally non-hyperelliptic,
  changes the asymptotics, and the known routes need extension-field or special
  curve structure. Assertion from literature, [pcc:unverified].

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- None from this item. Previous open threads (2026-11-14): rootfind-subtract
  GPU PR, m = 3 on GPU, validator check of the IC report.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- None.

### What was done
- Owner (2026-11-14): run the frozen `research/fes_k13_validated_20261112` measurement on Runpod, because the AWS isolab budget is exhausted (1,585.60 of 1,300 USD for the month).
- Inputs: the smoke binary built from e9d5dbdccd (sha256 8f9576b5…e5130f4, the same value as `smoke/build.json`), and `spec.json` (sha256 327e0bb4…a9d76c, the same value as `protocol.json` `links.spec_sha256`). Both were staged at `s3://isolab-results-104186811093-us-east-2/runpod/fes-k13-validated-20261112-a1/inputs/` and fetched by the pod through presigned GET URLs. The pod checks both hashes before it runs anything.
- Pod `buxbpz0nckybva`: Runpod CPU, `cpu3c`, 4 vCPU, `ubuntu:24.04`, 0.12 USD/h. It runs `ecbench host --json`, `ecbench run`, and `ecbench verify --replay-all --exit-code`, then PUTs `results.tgz` to the same prefix.
- Bench record `benchmarks/records/20261115T0009Z-fes-k13-runpod.json` opened. Resume checkpoint pushed (`20261115T000851Z-b61a431d279d`).

### Decisions made
- Runpod replaces the protocol's isolab L2 route. This is a disclosed deviation: a Runpod pod is shared hardware with no L2 admission, so its wall figures are not admitted. S is a deterministic count of code, workload and seed (README point 2), so the S values do not depend on the host.
- The binary needs glibc 2.39, hence `ubuntu:24.04`. The pod falls back to `--allow-busy` only if `ecbench run` refuses, and logs it if it does.

- `examples/b0_pdp_sat_baseline.rs`: five-arm harness (oracle, native, native-aa, cms, cadical); exports the solved instance (`to_dimacs_xor` / `to_dimacs`), verifies every SAT model against `symmetrised_s4_eval` and the subspace, records `R = conflicts/T_enum` and `R₃ = conflicts/C(2^ℓ,3)`.

Median R over the 16 uniform targets: native 10.88, native-aa 10.76, cadical 17.9, cms 36.4 (per-target CMS spread 2×–20×). `2^ℓ/3 = 10.7`, so native sits at ≈ 1.1 conflicts per *triple*, consistent with the survey's reading. `[pcc:unverified]` (dry run, L0, not a row).
- ℓ = 6 run started in the background.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Primary yardstick changed in the protocol from `R` (pairs) to `R₃` (triples) with `R` beside it: the incumbent is `R₃ ≈ 1` and `≈ 2^ℓ/3` behind the pair oracle; predictions rewritten accordingly before any ℓ ≥ 7 row.
- Candidate added for test before 3.1/3.2: a chained-S₃ encoding with branch priority on x₁, x₂ only, since after those are assigned the residual is affine over GF(2) and Gauss–Jordan should decide t and x₃ with no search (expected `R ≈ 1`, i.e. parity with the pair oracle in counts). Checking first whether the repo's chain encoder was already run this way.

- Harness arms `chain`/`chain-p12` (repository chain encoder, both identity patterns, `x₁≤x₂≤x₃`): ℓ = 5 median R 262 / 160 (vs native 10.9): the AND gates hide the linearity.
- **New solver** `examples/pdp_lin_cdcl.rs` (~600 lines, standard library): CDCL (2-watched literals, 1UIP, VSIDS with priority set) + XOR engine (full Gauss–Jordan re-elimination per fixpoint, combination tracking for exact explanations) + **field-product gates** `z = x·y` over F_{2^n} that inject `n` guarded XOR rows when one operand block is assigned and withdraw them on backtrack. `ChainInstance::build` encodes `S₃(x₁,x₂,t)`, `S₃(t,x₃,x_R)` with blocks x1,x2,x3,t,p,q,v,w,a and an optional lex comparator `x₁ ≤ x₂`.
- Harness arms `lin`, `lin-nolex`. ℓ = 5: median R 1.203 / 2.138 (native 10.875), 0 disagreements, 0 unverified, planted 4/4; 5.9 s for 20 targets × 3 arms. ℓ = 6: R 1.465 / 2.431 (native 21.94 = R₃ 1.095), clean. Every uniform refutation has R ≥ 1 (lin min 1.203 at ℓ = 5): no learnt clause excludes more than one pair. `[pcc:unverified]`
- `protocol.json` (experiment.protocol/v1, claims B1–B5), `analyze.py` (stdlib; SVG figures; smoke-tested on merged ℓ = 5 rows), `run_rows.sh`, `FREEZE.sha256`. Bench record opened: `benchmarks/records/20261115T0359Z-b0-pdp-sat-baseline.json`. `.resume/RESUME.md` rewritten for this effort.

- lin prediction set to R ∈ [1.0, 1.7] trending to ≈ 1.5 from the mechanism (half the pairs' quadratics in t have two roots; each root's x₃ fails separately), replacing the first guess [1.1, 1.4] after the ℓ = 6 check read 1.46. Set before any row.

## 2026-11-15T00:10:00Z — Q&A: why DLP solvers are faster on genus ≥ 2 Jacobians

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Answered a conceptual question in chat; no code, data or experiment touched.
- Summarised: Mumford decomposition gives a natural smoothness notion with
  constant probability 1/g! and a q-sized factor base against a q^g group;
  Gaudry / Thériault / GTTD Õ(q^{2-2/g}) beats rho q^{g/2} for g ≥ 3 [pcc:unverified] and ties
  at g = 2; Diem's line-based relations give Õ(q) on non-hyperelliptic genus 3
  (plane quartics), Diem–Kochinke Õ(q^{2-2/(g-1)}) for g ≥ 4; Smith's trigonal
  isogeny transfers ~18.6% of hyperelliptic genus-3 instances to that case;
  large genus goes L(1/2) then L(1/3) (Enge–Gaudry, EGT, Diem), which is what
  makes Weil descent (GHS) dangerous for E(F_{q^n}).
- Sources checked: answered from standard literature; KB not queried
  (conceptual question, no lead or novelty claim made).

### Decisions made (and why)

- Flagged for the program: a prime-field ECDLP transferred into genus 2 gains
  no exponent (Õ(q) vs rho Õ(q)); only genus ≥ 3, ideally non-hyperelliptic,
  changes the asymptotics, and the known routes need extension-field or special
  curve structure. Assertion from literature, [pcc:unverified].

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- None from this item. Previous open threads (2026-11-14): rootfind-subtract
  GPU PR, m = 3 on GPU, validator check of the IC report.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- None.

  (c0, c1, c2 and the six Veronese quadrics), rho, the four points of X over t, and `image_points`: the two points
  of X over an affine point P of H under the correspondence R of eq. (11), in F_{p^2}, with a rational/conjugate flag.
- `cargo test --release --lib smith_trigonal`: 6 passed (4 s), rerun by the main session. The F_37 Example 1 of the paper
  reproduces G, all four deltas and the printed equations of X; the derived delta_2 equals the printed one and
  `smith_prop24::smith_deltas`; every image point satisfies the model, eq. (8), b22 = rho^2 and y = (b02 + b12 x + b22 x^2)/rho
  on Example 1 plus 30 random Smith-positive curves (p = 37, 101, 211); exactly 2 points per P, 4 per fibre; two
  independent counts of #X over U agree. [pcc:unverified: unit-test output]

  model, and the fibres of X over t = infinity and triple-root fibres. Without psi no logarithm can be carried to a
  plane quartic, so no transfer cost is measured; this is stated as the limit, not hidden.

- Owner (2026-11-14): run the frozen `research/fes_k13_validated_20261112` measurement on Runpod, because the AWS isolab budget is exhausted (1,585.60 of 1,300 USD for the month).
- Inputs: the smoke binary built from e9d5dbdccd (sha256 8f9576b5…e5130f4, the same value as `smoke/build.json`), and `spec.json` (sha256 327e0bb4…a9d76c, the same value as `protocol.json` `links.spec_sha256`). Both were staged at `s3://isolab-results-104186811093-us-east-2/runpod/fes-k13-validated-20261112-a1/inputs/` and fetched by the pod through presigned GET URLs. The pod checks both hashes before it runs anything.
- Pod `buxbpz0nckybva`: Runpod CPU, `cpu3c`, 4 vCPU, `ubuntu:24.04`, 0.12 USD/h. It runs `ecbench host --json`, `ecbench run`, and `ecbench verify --replay-all --exit-code`, then PUTs `results.tgz` to the same prefix.
- Bench record `benchmarks/records/20261115T0009Z-fes-k13-runpod.json` opened. Resume checkpoint pushed (`20261115T000851Z-b61a431d279d`).

### Decisions made
- Runpod replaces the protocol's isolab L2 route. This is a disclosed deviation: a Runpod pod is shared hardware with no L2 admission, so its wall figures are not admitted. S is a deterministic count of code, workload and seed (README point 2), so the S values do not depend on the host.
- The binary needs glibc 2.39, hence `ubuntu:24.04`. The pod falls back to `--allow-busy` only if `ecbench run` refuses, and logs it if it does.

### Open threads
- Collect results, terminate the pod, run the independent replay on this container (a different host class), the validator report and band, write VALIDATION.json, then the report, PDF, benchmarks entry and PR.

### Errors
- Extracting the whole e9d5dbdccd tree filled this container's disk allowance. I removed the partial extract and three finished scratch directories, and reused the existing smoke binary instead of rebuilding.

## 2026-11-15T02:45:00Z — Effective degree is a forecast point, not a bound (wording fix after #4208)

  targets at (31,7), (37,8), (47,13), (53,15), (59,16), (61,16) with effective degree 2 and MutantXL solving degree 3.

  effective degree is a forecast point, not an upper bound. No behaviour change.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Kept `min(semi-regular, effective)` as the prior point: it held on all 15,543 measured random-target systems with
  `k ≥ 7`, the breaking targets (≥ 4 decompositions in V×V) are rare among random points (heuristic `2^(2k−1−n)`),
  and the Kaplan–Meier update corrects the point once solves exist. Only the "bound" wording was wrong. `[pcc:unverified]`

## R1c launched: local plus RunPod (Iso Lab blocked by the monthly budget) (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

The owner chose R1c with a $20 ceiling. Froze `r1_carry/PROTOCOL_R1C.md` and `src/bin/r1c.rs`
(commit ac299c6c), built static binaries, published `isolab/tasks@sha256:d6b31c88…`, committed the
command request (629b20a8), passed check-experiment-git and pushed a resume checkpoint. Then ran the
frozen binaries in two places:
- locally (Intel, 4 vCPU);
- on RunPod pod o7cujyw0f3pxbt (cpu5c, 8 vCPU, AMD EPYC 4564P, 05:22:51–05:25:24Z, about $0.01,
  deleted with HTTP 204), with binaries and results moved through presigned S3 URLs.

Arm A is byte-identical across the hosts. Arm B agrees on every value; only the tied argmax masks
differ. All frozen null bounds are met [pcc:unverified]. Report §12, graph `s6_r1c_by_curve.svg`,
PDF rebuilt.

### Decisions made (and why)

Did not launch on Iso Lab: the monthly account budget was exceeded ($1,851 against $1,300), and the
owner chose a local run, then RunPod. P2 turned out degenerate (x ≡ 0 mod 100 forces
bit0 xor bit1 = 0). This is reported as a deviation, with a post-hoc non-degenerate check
(`src/bin/r1c_p2.rs`, x ≡ 0 mod 101) that detects the weight-2 plant at (3,1) with χ² 124–192.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Masks of weight ≥ 3 and cryptographic sizes are untested. The published ECR image and the request
remain unused. RunPod billing had not posted yet; the estimate is $0.012.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The runpodctl curl|bash install was denied by auto mode; used the RunPod REST API v2 directly.
Presigning needed boto3 (installed). The first S6 statistic ignored marginals; this was caught in a
toy smoke test and replaced by a 2×2 independence χ² before any frozen data.

about $0.11, deleted with HTTP 204). Results: Part 1 (weight ≤ 3, T = 2.8M) S6 24.85–33.41 against a
bound of 42; weight-2 and weight-3 plants detected at their planted pairs. Part 2 (p ≈ 2²⁴–2²⁵)
S6 21.0 and 22.8 against 33; S7 8–9; S3 ≤ 2. All bounds met [pcc:unverified]. Report §13, PDF rebuilt.

Reported the 4-of-6 test-over-shuffled pattern and the largest value 33.41 (family-wise p ≈ 0.07)
rather than omitting them. Neither is significant.

Lead search: the Fable synthesist was stopped by safeguards ([cyber]); the Sonnet IC-engine generator
is still running. R1c and R1d cost about $0.12 of the $20 ceiling.

The protocol estimated T at about 6.8M; the actual value is 2.8M (21 varying x-bits), so the frozen
bound is conservative. The stop hook flagged phantom changes in the decrypted crypto-autoresearcher
checkout (stat only, unverifiable without the key); nothing was committed there.

  (`VALIDATION_REVIEW.md`, committed unchanged). 88 certificates re-verified with its own code; every
  record replayed on a CPU host through the emulation worker; no number refuted. Ten statements
  corrected or qualified in REPORT.md (gpu_targets_unused 3–984; set-up and LA ranges; the backend is
  not recorded; j ≥ i argument empirical; r-scaling heuristic; L0 and no IC A/A; wall distance to rho
  115–787×; start 23:00Z; prefetch cause a hypothesis); `analyze.py` best-ratio label fixed and
  RESULTS.md regenerated from the tarball; REPORT.pdf regenerated.

  arbitrary abscissa set, no j ≥ i filter; session API takes the probe set; worker protocol RFS2;
  `rootfind_serve --daemon SOCKET` (one CUDA context, one connection at a time; `rf_warmup`).
- `ic_framework/rootfind_gpu.rs`: `rootfind-subtract-gpu:m=3` (device probe set = the
  negation-folded pair table's abscissae, `PairTable::map_abscissae`); `worker=spawn|daemon`
  parameter; overflowing batches split; worker connection thread-local.

  pipelines; a parameter-refusal test.

  analyze.py, RESUME.md).

### Decisions made (and why)
- Daemon mode is an explicit method parameter (`worker=daemon`) so records say which mode ran; its
  one-time start is logged and reported beside the records, not hidden.
- The job aborts unless the daemon and the worker report the CUDA backend (the backend is not a
  record field, so a CPU-host replay reads the same record).
- Specs committed and pushed before the pod run; the benchmark record initialised on a clean tree.

- m = 3 mutation control result; ecbench build; pod run; report; PR.

- Session create read `s->fbx` before it was filled (probe-mode refactor); fixed before any build.

## 2026-11-15T07:30Z — Wave 3: closure depth lemma (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- **Depth lemma**, written up in `research/kummer_wave3_20261115/depth/README.md` `[pcc:unverified]`. In the round-indexed HKY closure, 1 cannot enter before round 2t (m = 2) or 2t+2 (m = 3). It explains why λ is a closure pseudo-solution and gives the criterion: `G_t(c) < 2t` implies `d_F > c`.
- `depth.py` sweeps (raw/*.jsonl):
  - m = 2: r* = 2t on 28/28 cells.
  - m = 3: r* = 6 at t = 2 (= bound), 9 at t = 3 (bound 8).
  - m = 2, c = 4: saturation depth G = 11 at t = 6, 7, 8 and 9, flat in t. This reproduces the d_F step at t = 6.
- Frozen predictions (`PREDICTIONS.md`, committed first): P1 held at t = 8 and 9; P2 held (r* = 16 at t = 8, c = 5); P3 not run.
- `cargo build --release --example pkm_tower_pilot` succeeds online (only `--offline` fails). Rust hky gives m = 3 Kummer t = 4: d_F = 6 on 2/2 draws (34 s each). An m = 3, t = 5 run is in progress.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Stopped the t = 10 runs: at p = 40961, K² ≈ 10⁶ grid points leave no inconsistent draws, and lab A's closure needs p < 2^16 for exact float arithmetic.
- Ran the Rust pilot with `--kinds kummer --random 1` after two parallel draws reached about 13 GB.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Conjecture D (G_t(c) ≤ B(c) uniformly in t): test B(5) at t ≥ 10. This needs either the Rust closure instrumented to report rounds or exact integer linear algebra with p > 2^20.
- The Rust m = 3, t = 5 result; t = 6 is not run yet (memory).

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- While freeing memory I killed the wrong pilot process (the t = 5 run instead of the finished t = 4 one). Restarted t = 5 alone.
- `sleep` chaining was blocked; switched to Monitor.

## 2026-11-15T06:00Z — remove the never-run B0 benchmark record (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Owner's ask: "Close the open B0 record so 4238 can merge."
- `benchmarks/records/20261115T0359Z-b0-pdp-sat-baseline.json` was opened at the B0 protocol freeze (#4225, dec928b649). It had no commands, metrics or raw data, and its entry directory `benchmarks/20261115-b0-pdp-sat-baseline` was never committed. That kept `records` red on main and on every PR.
- `benchrec.py close --status invalid` refuses this record. A closed record must name a recorded command and an existing entry directory, and this one has neither.
- Removed the open record instead (`git rm`): the checker allows that, since it treats only closed records as immutable. Then `benchrec.py report` regenerated `benchmarks/progress/`. `benchrec.py report --check`: current.
- Done on the #4238 branch, so that PR carries the fix to main.

### Decisions made (and why)

- No command was invented to satisfy `close`. Closing would have created a record of a run that never happened. Removing a record that never ran loses no data.
- The B0 run, when it happens, opens a new record under `benchrec.py init`.

## 01:50Z — L2 done: even-degree Smith census (Claude; subagent-run, deliverables checked)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- `src/bin/smith_even_census.rs`, `research/smith_even_census_20261115/` (protocol frozen before data, hash OK).
  5,852 squarefree degree-8 curves over p = 31, 47, 67, 97 with exact #J by zeta over F_p^k (routine verified
  30/30 against Cantor orders on degree-7 and on degree-8-with-a-rational-root curves, both twists).
- Measured [pcc:unverified]: pass 0.174 overall, 0.140 admitted (h <= 8), 0 of 168 prime-order curves (Wilson [0, 0.022]);
  type (8) passes at 0.249 but is never of odd order.

### Decisions made (and why)
- My brief's 2-rank formula for all-even factor degrees was wrong (k, not k - 1: the Frobenius-swapped complementary
  even subset is a rational class; genus-1 check: an irreducible quartic has a rational 2-torsion point). The subagent
  corrected it in the protocol BEFORE data and preregistered E1b/E3'; E3 as I wrote it failed, as the correction predicts.
- Consequence, now for both models: a prime-order genus-3 hyperelliptic Jacobian over F_p (odd p) never has a
  rational tractable subgroup (odd model: type (7,1); even model: types (1,7), (3,5); none admits a stable pairing).

## 02:45Z — L1 done: the canonical map psi: X -> C and the first native Smith DLP transfer (Claude; Fable-built, re-tested here)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- `src/cryptanalysis/smith_canonical.rs` (new; `pub mod` line in mod.rs), study `research/smith_canonical_20261115/`
  (PROTOCOL.md frozen 02:31:04Z, sha 519b465a; RESULTS.md; summary.json; raw cell logs; REPORT.pdf rendered here).
- Route (not the adjoint route I suggested): H^0(X, Omega) = traces along Smith's correspondence R of x^i dx/y
  (an isogeny of degree prime to p is an isomorphism on differentials), which gives psi(t; b) = T(t)·(b0:b1:b2) with
  T a symmetric 3x3 matrix over F_p[t], and C as the unique quartic through enough image points (rejected unless
  exactly one quartic, no conic, no cubic). Classes on C through `plane_quartic_pic` (`class_from_conics`), including
  conjugate point pairs via real/imaginary conics; `transfer_divisor` moves any Mumford class of H onto rational points over U.
- Re-run here: `cargo test --release --lib smith_canonical` 9 passed, 1 ignored; rustfmt clean. Example 1 (F_37): fitted
  C is projectively equivalent to Smith's printed C (one projectivity found by exhaustive search), #C(F_37^k) = #H(F_37^k)
  for k = 1, 2, 3, and the paper's k = 22359 transfers: Psi([k]D) = [k]Psi(D), BSGS on C returns 22359.
- Subagent's frozen sweep: 70 Smith-positive curves (p = 37, 101, 211, deg 7 and 8): quartic found and smooth on every
  curve, zeta(C) = zeta(H) on every curve where counted, principal divisors -> 0 (497/497, perturbed non-zero 497/497),
  representation independence / additivity / #J-annihilation 500/500 each, 2-torsion killed iff in S (246/246), DLP
  recovered 50/50 (on 4 curves modulo ord D / 2, the lost 2-part being in the kernel). [pcc:unverified]
- My own spot check, a fresh 5-curve cell at p = 37 (seed 777): 4 pass every check; 1 curve has a hyperelliptic X
  (images on a conic), correctly refused — the subagent's sweep met none in 70 curves, so "no hyperelliptic X" is a
  sample statement, not a property.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Accept the trace-of-differentials construction: it is justified (isogeny prime to p is an iso on differentials) and
  verified against the paper's printed curve, zeta and transfer, which is stronger than any route-internal check.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- deg-8 H: canonical map verified, transfer not (no real-model J_H arithmetic). Fibres outside U not evaluated.
- Measure the transferred DLP cost: Diem on C (quartic_ladder arms) against rho on J_H for Smith-positive instances.
- Independent validator replay of the sweep; L3 still running.

  amendment 1). `research/genus_ladder_scale_20261115/{scale_g2.jsonl,scale_g2b.jsonl,g2_analysis.json,tables_g2.md,fig_*_g2.*}`.
- Measured [pcc:unverified]: exponents in p over 8209..2^24, FPM primary: rho 0.975 [0.908, 1.037], 2LP+pre 1.004
  [0.991, 1.020], trials 0.980; full base FPM 1.97 (8209..262147). Ratio 2LP/rho (FPM mid) 3.3, 2.0, 2.2, 4.6, 3.9, 5.7,
  3.0, 3.7, 3.6, 4.6, 1.7, 5.1 from 8209 to 2^24: no trend, no crossing; LA share of the 2LP arm 0.08–0.20.
- Scorecard so far: X1 held; X2 held (both exponents at 1); X3 FAILED (ratio not 2x smaller at 2^24); X4 FAILED
  (no crossing: distance remaining 3–5x, flat in p); X6 FAILED (LA share stays below 0.2: cycles in the genus-2
  large-prime graph are short because a 2-LP relation carries no base column, so the solve is cheap and the cost is
  the relation walk itself). X5 awaits the genus-3 cells.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Report genus 2 as a clean negative with the mechanism: at genus 2 the 2LP method and rho both scale as p^1 and the
  method is a constant 3–5x behind in this instrument; the gap is in the walk (one Cantor step per trial, half of
  the trials split, and each split relation is one edge of a graph that needs about p/2 edges), not in the algebra.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Genus-3 cells running; close the bench record; RESULTS.md; PR.

## 2026-11-15 ~05:00Z (heading time corrected; originally mis-stamped 05:10Z) — Grevlex solving-degree bounds for the normalized two-summand Kummer chain systems: setup, engine, admissible family (Claude, main session)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Task (owner): rigorous bounds `ck−C ≤ sd(I_k) ≤ Ak+B` for the normalized two-summand elliptic-curve chain systems over `F_p` (depth `k`, `2^(k+1) | p−1`), with pinned definitions, an admissible family with proved unitness, certificates beyond depth 14, and Lean proofs. Study directory: `research/chain_sd_bounds_20261115/`.
- Oriented in the repository: the target is the PKM Kummer chain at arity 2 (`research/notes/index-calculus/RESEARCH_PKM_TOWER_ORACLE.md`; family (T) in `research/prime_field_ic_closure_20261112/finish/theorem/PROOF.md`; engine rules `kummer` in `research/leads_followup_20261114/item09_velu_tower/tower_engine.py`). Prior committed values (lane P, `research/leads_20261112/`): LFD = 6 at k = 10..13, 7 at k = 14, ≥ 7 at k = 15; law `⌊(k+14)/4⌋`; written lower bound of slope 1/14 (ml-cryptanalysis `docs/proofs/N8-growth.md`, not readable here: `add_repo` for `isogeny-labs/ml-cryptanalysis` was denied by the permission classifier, both push and read).
- Wrote the native engine `native/` (Rust, `chainsd`): exact `F_p` arithmetic (p < 2^23, lazily reduced rows), squarefree-monomial layout (characters `x1^a x2^b`, weight = popcount), the degree-bounded closure `V_D` with a full `verify_closed` sweep, grid-zero (unit) test in O(N) via the quadratic in `x2` cross-checked against O(N²) brute force, boundary-point evaluation test, basis dump, instance search. `cargo build --release`: 4 dead-code warnings, no errors.
- Parity gate against the committed N8 profile (instance p 12289, A 4021, B 9917, t 4408): `dim V_3(k) = 2k+1` (k = 4..9: 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19) and `dim V_4(k) = 163, 386, 605, 829, 1061, 1301` (k = 4..9), `verify_closed: bad = 0` throughout. Also `dim V_4 = 1549, 1805, 2069` at k = 10, 11, 12 (new; fits `4k² + 172k − 571`). LFD on this instance: 4, 4, 4, 4, 5, 5 at k = 2..7 (k = 8, 9 are proper instances: 2 and 16 grid zeros). Weight profile of V_4 at k = 6..12: exactly two weight-2 elements, the generator and one k-independent 17-term element supported on exponents ≤ 4.
- Boundary-evaluation test (functionals `f ↦ f(ξ1, ξ2)` with the standard lift, `(ξ1, ξ2)` on `S_3 = 0` with one coordinate in `μ_N`): annihilate all of `V_D` for D ≤ 4 at k = 7, 8 and D ≤ 3 everywhere (k ≥ 3), but not `V_4` at k = 6 (188 of 605 rows fail) although `1 ∉ V_4(6)`. So these functionals are not the exact annihilator of the closure.
- Unitness. Found and checked (by exhaustive grid enumeration) that the apparent rule "j = 1728 curve with a non-square target is unit" is a theorem: with `2^(k+1) | p−1`, `E: y² = x³ + a x`, `t` a non-square and `t³ + a t` a nonzero square, the system is a unit ideal (proof via the explicit identities `S_3(v1,v2,c) = (v1−v2)²(c − x(P1+P2))(c − x(P1−P2))`, `x1 x2 x(P1+P2) (x2−x1)² = (x1 y2 − x2 y1)²`, and the `F_{p²}` square/non-square case split). Explicit family: `a = n²(n−1), t = n` for any non-square `n`. Checked unit: p 12289 (n 11; k 4, 6, 8, 9, 11), p 65537 (n 3; k 9, 12, 15), p 786433 (n 5; k 17); the square `n` (7, 13, 14, 17 mod 12289) give 653–688 grid zeros, as the theorem predicts. A tempting alternative (twist-point target) was refuted by data: ≈ N²/p grid zeros (16,168–16,570 at k = 15, p 65537), because sums of two twist points are twist points.
- Toolchain: Lean 4.34.1 from `releases.lean-lang.org` into `/opt/lean`, Mathlib `d13f23b7` (tag v4.34.1) cloned to `/opt/mathlib4` with `lake exe cache get` (8908 files). `lean/` project (`lakefile.toml` pinning that rev, manifest copied from `research/p2_halving_carry_20261101`) builds a `ZMod` smoke theorem in 16 s with the packages symlinked to `/opt/mathlib4`.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Pinned system: ring `F_p[y_{1,0..k−1}, y_{2,0..k−1}]`, grevlex with `y_{1,0} > … > y_{1,k−1} > y_{2,0} > … > y_{2,k−1}`, generators `y_{s,j+1} − y_{s,j}²`, `y_{s,k−1}² − 1`, `S_3(y_{1,0}, y_{2,0}, t)`; no field equations (they have degree p > any degree considered and are inert in `V_{F,c}` for c < p). Algorithm: Caminata–Gorla's degree-by-degree Macaulay closure (`V_{F,c}`, MutantXL), solving degree = the degree at which it stops.
- Work in the quotient algebra `F_p[(Z/N)²]` with the popcount weight; the equivalence `V_{F,c} = NF(V_{F,c}) ⊕ J_{≤c}` is to be written out (tower generators are a grevlex Gröbner basis with coprime leading terms).
- All code native (AGENTS.md "no Python"); exploration prototypes in the scratchpad only.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Lower bound proof (own argument needed; the N8 slope-1/14 proof is unreadable from here) and the depth-15 certificates (closure at D = 6 has 768,212 columns; dense is out of reach on this host, so a structural certificate is required).
- Running: `V_5` at k = 10 on the N8 instance; LFD of the family instances (p 12289 n 11, p 65537 n 3) at k = 2..8.

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Certificate format `chainsd-cert/v1` (engine env `CHAINSD_CERT=FILE`): header `p k kshift uni D`, the reduced generator, then one line `s v r` per closure step (variable `v` times basis row `r`), then `end rank R one_in_V b`. Independent checker `native/checker/chaincheck.c` (C, `cc -O2`): replays the steps with its own exact `F_p` linear algebra, enforces the degree budget on every step (source row weight `≤ D−1`), requires every step to be a new basis element, decides `1 ∈ span`, and for lower certificates runs the full closure sweep (every weight-`≤ D−1` row times every variable reduces to zero). JSON verdict, exit 0 iff verified.
- Family instance p 12289, n 11 (A 1210, B 0, t 11): generated and verified 15 certificates: lower at D = 3 (k = 2..5) and D = 4 (k = 6..9), upper at D = 4 (k = 2..5) and D = 5 (k = 6..8); all `verified: true`, ranks 5, 7, 9, 11 (D = 3), 605, 829, 1061, 1301 (D = 4), full rank at the upper degree. k = 9 D = 5 and the p 65537, n 3, k = 10, D = 5 certificates are still being generated.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Count a source subgroup only when the transfer is a nonzero algebraic map. For ordinary curves not isogenous to a subfield curve, Diem's simplicity theorem forces genus at least seven for any Jacobian target of a nonzero algebraic transfer over F_2.

### What was done
- Froze `research/curve25519_isogeny_scaling_20261115/PROTOCOL.md` (`1bd45ffc3b`); dumped 3,000 t=5 candidates (7,725,157 tower ops) and chose seed 1550 (c = 26) before its arm; ran its blind arm; t = 6 pilot then 8 blind arms (seeds 101-108); `analyse.py`. All arms exit 0.
- Verified by script: pairs law (b(b-1)/2 per #E, no fitted constant) supported on seeds 0-20 and bottom 8, not on top 8 (146 vs 122.2, interval to 144); at t = 6, 315 vs 306.7. t = 6 costs 3.06e6 mul-adds per target (17.3x) so hits per mul-add are 0.22x; H23 and H25 not supported. Seed 1550: 30 hits, 8.476 per 1e9 [5.719, 12.101]; H26-H28 supported. `[pcc:unverified]`.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Raising t is not carried forward (cost per target grows faster than hits). Lifts dropped, descent/torsion skipped, as in the protocol.
